# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, use the MCP server's `load_workspace_dependencies` tool (`mcp__codex_app__load_workspace_dependencies`) to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to conversations in Codex. Use "chat" when referring to conversations in the product. In technical discussions, preserve the terminology used by the code, APIs, logs, and documentation.
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_pinned`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.

## Pre-stable APIs and released Session data

Public APIs are pre-stable; update every consumer. Follow [version/status](docs/session-format-status.md) and [type acknowledgements](docs/cookbook/reviewing-persistence-type-changes.md). [Adjacent migration](.agents/notes/implemented/architecture/2026-08-31-released-session-format-migrations.md) may add a version-named successor but never move, overwrite, or delete committed generations; predecessors imply neither fallback nor downgrade support. SQLite uses monotonic `SCHEMA_VERSION`.

Acknowledge [declared persistence-type changes](docs/cookbook/reviewing-persistence-type-changes.md).

**Application launch.** Only `dsh` profiles launch supported Node apps; package bins, demos, and public SDK argv escapes are forbidden ([rule](docs/architecture.md#application-launch)).

## Environment / running state
- Installed app is running: `C:\Users\chenfei\AppData\Local\Prism Agent\dsh-desktop.exe` (PID 6940), bundled `runtime\dsh` hub on `127.0.0.1:3081` (PID 29380, token in `~/.prism/desktop-server.json`).
- Repo `apps/desktop/runtime/` **does not exist** — it is produced by `node scripts/bundle-runtime.mjs --platform win`, then `pnpm tauri build` (NSIS, currentUser, installs to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Prism Agent`). The installed exe is from 2026/10/1 22:51.
- **Critical**: the installed app serves `runtime\dsh\node_modules\@deepseek-ai\dsh-web-frontend\dist\index.html`, which was built **before** `scripts/bundle-runtime.mjs` gained its `hold`/`ready` splash bridge. So the shipped shell (`apps/desktop/ui/dist/app.js`) waits the full 20 s `READY_TIMEOUT_MS` before hiding the splash. That means the splash is the first thing the user sees for ~20 s and is the layer most likely implicated in complaint 2.
- Browser binding available: in-app browser (iab, browserId `2`), tab `1`, used for verification. Blocked from `file://` URLs — local preview was served over HTTP instead.

## Findings
### Splash / logo (issue 1)
- Splash markup lives in `apps/desktop/ui/dist/index.html` (hand-maintained shell frontend, no build step; `ui/package.json` is trivial). Old markup was a plain orange rounded square:
  `<div class="splash-mark" aria-hidden="true"></div>` with `.splash-mark { width:44px; height:44px; border-radius:12px; background:var(--accent); opacity:0.9 }` in `apps/desktop/ui/dist/style.css`.
- Brand SVG sources: `design-assets/app-icons/prism-icon.svg` (orange rounded-square plate + white hexagon + orange lattice; used for the store/installer/general app icon, present as `official-512.png`), `prism-icon-badge.svg` (white plate + black lattice → actual Windows `icon.ico` / taskbar / titlebar), `prism-icon-small.svg`, `prism-mark.svg`, `prism-icon-square.svg`, `prism-mark-android.svg`. `design-assets/app-icons/README.md` documents the geometry and that `packages/client/ui-primitives/src/PrismLogo.tsx` must stay in sync.
- Installer art (`icons/installer-header.bmp`, `installer-sidebar.bmp`) uses the black-on-white badge on a dark navy plate.
- App-in code uses `PrismLogo` (currentColor hexagon lattice) and `PrismBadgeLogo` (white plate + black lattice) from `packages/client/ui-primitives/src/{PrismLogo,PrismBadgeLogo}.tsx`.

### Blur/veil (issue 2)
- Probe of the live app in the browser found **zero** elements with `backdrop-filter`, and only `DropOverlay`-style full-viewport elements; no stuck overlay was actually mounted at probe time (page had fully loaded, `#splash` already removed).
- Prime suspect for a white veil: `packages/client/ui-attachment/src/DropOverlay.module.css` — `.mask { position: fixed; inset: 0; z-index: 1000; background-color: var(--dsw-alias-bg-mask-drop) /* rgba(255,255,255,.7) */; backdrop-filter: blur(10px) }`, rendered through a body portal by `DropOverlay.tsx` while a file drag is over the page. If the document-level `dragleave`/`dragend` reset in `packages/client/ui-attachment/src/client/drop-events.ts` is missed, this layer stays up = "一层模糊的一层在应用上层".
- Other full-viewport blurred layers: `packages/client/ui-primitives/src/Modal.module.css` (`.mask`, `--dsw-mask-blur: blur(2px)` + rgba(0,0,0,.24) — dark, not white), `OnboardingSurface.module.css` (`.onboardingMask`, `blur(2px)`, dark), `packages/client/ui-dockkit/src/components/dockkit.module.css` `.dockScrim` (`blur(6px)`, only during dock drag), `SettingsRoot.module.css`, `ImageLightbox.module.css`.

## Task
User (Chinese, writes 中文) reported two problems with the running Prism Agent desktop app (fork of DeepSeek Harness = "Prism Agent", repo `C:\Users\chenfei\Documents\GitHub\prism-agent`):
1. 启动的 logo 没有换成默认 logo — splash logo wasn't the default app logo.
2. 启动以后感觉有一层模糊的一层在应用上层 — a blurry/veil layer sits over the app after launch.

A later user message clarified: **"启动logo用新的那个logo啊"** — use the NEW logo for the startup splash.

**Second user message (current instruction):** `可以，那你 pnpm run build + 重新 bundle runtime + 重打安装包` — approved doing the full rebuild chain: `pnpm run build` + re-bundle runtime + re-package the installer.

## Environment
- Shell: PowerShell. Sandbox `workspace-write`; escalations used throughout (network, GUI launch, cargo).
- Node: system node v24.19.0 (`C:\Users\chenfei\Tools\node\node-v23.9.0-win-x64` on PATH via pnpm shim; node.exe in PATH is v23.9.0). Repo requires ^22.19 || >=24.
- cargo/rustc 1.98.1 at `C:\Env\Rust\cargo\bin` (**not on PATH** — must prefix with `C:\Env\Rust\cargo\bin` or call `C:\Env\Rust\cargo\bin\cargo.exe`).
- Tauri CLI 2.11.4 lives only in the npm npx cache: `C:\Users\chenfei\AppData\Local\npm-cache\_npx\c0935828a8814453\node_modules\.bin\tauri.cmd`. `pnpm tauri build` is unusable (its `beforeBuildCommand` triggers `pnpm install --production` → network + TTY failures).
- RealAPI env: root `.env` with `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY`.

## Confirmed Root Causes
- **Splash logo**: splash markup in `apps/desktop/ui/dist/index.html` had a plain orange rounded square (`.splash-mark` with `background: var(--accent)`). Fixed by inlining the real app icon art.
- **"白纱" veil**: the shipped `runtime/` bundle's served page (`@deepseek-ai/dsh-web-frontend/dist/index.html`) has **no** `dsh-desktop-host` bridge, so the shell's `hold`/`ready` handshake never fires and the opaque white splash stays over the iframe for the full 20 s `READY_TIMEOUT_MS`. Second contributing bug: `bundle-runtime.mjs::injectDesktopHostBridge` starts with `if (html.includes('dsh-desktop-host')) return;`, and the pristine upstream `dist/index.html` now already contains a bridge script (notification/opener only, no `hold`/`ready`), so the new hold/ready bridge is never injected.

## Changes Already Made (uncommitted, in `apps/desktop/ui/dist/`)
1. `index.html` — replaced the orange-square `.splash-mark` with an inline SVG copy of `design-assets/app-icons/prism-icon-badge.svg` (white rounded plate `rect x=112 y=112 w=800 h=800 rx=176` + `feDropShadow` + black lattice `#1A1A1A`→`#050505`, `translate(512 512) scale(24) translate(-12 -12)`). Gradient ids renamed to `splash-mark-ink` / `splash-mark-shadow`. Comment names the vector source.
2. `style.css` — `.splash-mark` → `{ display:block; width:72px; height:72px }` (was 64px; no background).
3. `app.js` — added `const SPLASH_SETTLE_MS = 600;` and hardened `hideSplash()` so `.gone` is applied by a 600 ms timer as well as `transitionend`.

Verified: geometry matches the badge master (1 rect / 2 paths / 7 circles, all attribute strings identical); `node --check apps/desktop/ui/dist/app.js` passes; installed exe contains the new markup (checked the tauri codegen asset `target/release/build/dsh-desktop-5985d042c5f985df/out/tauri-codegen-assets/3ab22c7e…html` contains `splash-mark-ink`, and `3f1b33b0…js` contains `SPLASH_SETTLE_MS`).
